Thus the table has 25 PASS entries, no FAIL entry, and one explicitly
unproved target interface. The latter does not invalidate the finite-word
lemma. No suggested correction to the frozen object is necessary on the
basis of this audit.

## 1. Definitions, dependencies, and what is being checked

All vectors and norms are real Euclidean. A word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))
has finite length n>=0 and finite nonnegative entries. Physical GD is
x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L) grad f(x_t), where L>0. The normalized worst-case
value W_n(h) is the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)

over finite dimensions, convex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz
gradient and an attained minimum, minimizers x_star, and x_0!=x_star.
The project convention is W=2R, where R is the same supremum without
the leading factor2. A choice of the closest minimizer gives
a legal instance of this supremum; it must be proved closest if that
distance is asserted to be exact.

The original benchmark is U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, where C_0=B_0=1 and

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).

These definitions match the statement of accepted fact04219554e41d9750.
No ray estimate, restricted split recurrence, computed benchmark value,
or conditional seed conclusion from that fact is used here.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, whose full statement and proof were read,
fixes the ordered residual convention

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The audit derives Q_ij<=0 directly for the constructed functions. It does
not invoke that fact to delete incoming terminal rows or any prefix row.
The source paper is used to audit attribution and normalization. No
external lemma is an unchecked construction premise. All new reasoning
below is part of this single unverified audit, not a collection of accepted
intermediate facts.

For readability only, in the rest of this report c,d,T,H,A denote the
frozen draft's c_sp,d_sp,T_sp,H_sp,A_sp. Theta, D_i, ell_i, e_t and other
local letters retain the frozen draft's explicitly stated meanings in
this report; they do not redefine conflicting entries in the project
glossary. Coordinates of the global space are indexed0,...,k.

## 2. Primary-source normalization and applicability

The exact source is Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is (Almost)
Optimal for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152v2, September10,2026,
[primary HTML](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2).
Its Eq(1) divides by (L/2)||x_0-x_star||^2: its performance symbol equals
project W. Lemma2.2 states the weaker prefactor1/[4(1+s_tail)]. Section3.1
and AppendixA.3 Eq(40) construct F(x_n)=D^2/[4(1+s_tail)] with initial
point e_1 and minimizer0, and explicitly discard the extra factor2 from
Eq(1). The stronger prefactor1/[2(1+s_tail)] therefore belongs to the
construction, not the literal lemma statement.

AppendixA.1 gives the frozen H_Y; AppendixA.2 Eq(35) gives
C_Y=epsilon+(H_Y+1)/epsilon. Section3.1 and AppendixA.3 require local
steps h/2, local checkpoint b/2, local A=c_Y a/2, hence physical
b>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)) and a>=2C_Y/c_Y. Write B_0^Y=2(1+epsilon^(-2))
for this source threshold, distinct from the benchmark B_0=1.
Section3.2 additionally imposes a>=max{2,2B_0^Y+2}. These are the exact
hypotheses being compared.

The exact downloaded HTML and extracted mathematical text are preserved
under this audit directory's `downloads/2609.09152v2.{html,txt}`.
The definition, relevant statements, full AppendixA.1--A.3 proofs, and
the extra recursion floors were read. Matlas retrieval returned unrelated
small-step self-contraction and convex-hull results; none was imported.

## 3. Parameters and all supporting normals

Take exactly 0<kappa<=epsilon<=1/2 and define

    Theta=arctan(1/epsilon),
    c=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa Theta), d=1-kappa^2,
    T=(exp(d Theta/kappa)-1)/d,
    H=c[1+(1+kappa)T]-1, A=epsilon+(H+1)/epsilon.

Here 0<Theta<pi/2, c>1, d>=3/4, T>0, H>0, A>0, and all are finite.
The curve and its proposed normal are

    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    e_t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    R_sp(theta)=c exp(-kappa theta),
    v(theta)=R_sp(theta)e(theta),
    n_sp(theta)=e(theta)+kappa e_t(theta), 0<=theta<=Theta.

Writing v=(p,-q), the endpoint identity follows from
cos Theta=epsilon/sqrt(1+epsilon^2) and
sin Theta=1/sqrt(1+epsilon^2): v(0)=(c,0), v(Theta)=(epsilon,-1).
The derivatives are

    p'=-R_sp(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'= R_sp(cos theta-kappa sin theta)>=0.

The second sign follows from cot theta>=cot Theta=epsilon>=kappa,
with theta=0 handled directly. Thus epsilon<=p<=c, 0<=q<=1, and
q/p=tan theta<=1/epsilon. These signs are proved, not assumed for an
arbitrary curve outside this parameter range.

Let K be the convex hull of0 and the compact curve. For any two angles
theta,phi in the full interval, put Delta=phi-theta. Then

    <n_sp(theta),v(phi)>/R_sp(theta)
       = exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+kappa sin Delta).

The derivative with respect to Delta is
-(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta. It is nonnegative for Delta<=0
and nonpositive for Delta>=0 on [-Theta,Theta]; the maximum is1 at0.
Also <n_sp(theta),0>=0<R_sp(theta). Consequently

    <n_sp(theta),w-v(theta)><=0 for EVERY w in K.

This includes both endpoints, all nonadjacent curve points, and every
convex combination. The additional normal (0,1) at v(0) is valid because
K is in the closed lower half-plane. No local curvature test is being
substituted for global supporting inequalities.

## 4. Exact backward construction, including the cap

For any ordinary block alpha_1,...,alpha_m>=0 start with
r_m=v_m=v(Theta). Write r_j=(u_j,-y_j). Assume the frozen induction
invariants u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon. At a noncapped
state also write

    r_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e(theta_j)+kappa t_j e_t(theta_j)],
    t_j>=0.

This representation holds initially with t_m=0. For alpha=alpha_j,
the capped test is y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)c]. If it holds, set
mu=u_j+(alpha-1)c. Then mu>=y_j/kappa>0 and

    r_j+alpha v(0)-v(0)
       =mu n_sp(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j).

Both coefficients of the valid normals are nonnegative. Their sum
proves that v(0) is the projection of r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(0) on K.
The new point obeys y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-c). At any earlier step
alpha'>=0 this implies y_(j-1)<=kappa[u_(j-1)+(alpha'-1)c]. Hence
capping persists. There is no assumption that a huge ordinary step
must remain on the curved part.

If the cap test fails, a previously capped state is impossible. Define

    F_j(theta)=<r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)e_t(theta)>/R_sp(theta).

The current representation gives F_j(theta_j)=1, while failure of
the cap test gives F_j(0)=(u_j-y_j/kappa)/c<1-alpha. Direct differentiation
gives

    F_j'(theta)=(kappa+kappa^(-1))
                  (u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R_sp(theta)>0.

Since 1-alpha<=1, there is one and only one root
theta in(0,theta_j] with F_j(theta)=1-alpha. Moreover, if
Delta=theta_j-theta, then

    <r_j,e_t(theta)>
       =R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)sin Delta+kappa t_j cos Delta]>=0.

The root equation therefore says that
r_j+(alpha-1)v(theta) is a nonnegative multiple of n_sp(theta).
It proves the projection of r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(theta), and gives its
new representation with t_(j-1)>=0. This is an exact scalar definition,
not a solver invocation or a feasibility guess.

Finally the actual additions are
u_(j-1)=u_j+alpha p(theta), y_(j-1)=y_j+alpha q(theta).
Their signs preserve u>=epsilon and y>=1. The new ratio y/u is the
weighted average of y_j/u_j and q/p with nonnegative weights summing
to1, so it remains <=1/epsilon. This closes the induction. For alpha=0,
the noncapped root is theta_j and the entire state is unchanged; a
capped state remains unchanged too. No ordering assumption on the
magnitudes of alpha_j is used.

## 5. Uniform displacement bounds

Define P=sum_j alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),
Q=sum_j alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), and S=sum_j alpha_j. Then
r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), 0<=P<=cS, Q>=0, and P>=epsilon Q by the
pointwise curve bounds.

For two consecutive noncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new, let
Delta=theta_new-theta_old. Tangential components in the exact update
give

    t_old=B(Delta)+A_turn(Delta)t_new,
    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa,
    A_turn(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2,

    A_turn'-(d/kappa)A_turn
       =-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0,

so A_turn<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Also B<=Delta/kappa gives
1+dB<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Thus

    1+d t_old<=exp(d Delta/kappa)(1+d t_new).

The angle increments telescope to at most Theta-theta_j, irrespective
of the number of steps or their sizes. Starting at t_m=0 gives
t_j<=(exp(d(Theta-theta_j)/kappa)-1)/d<=T.
At every noncapped point,

    y_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)sin theta_j+kappa t_j cos theta_j]
       <=c[1+(1+kappa)T]=1+H.

A capped update has q=0 and leaves y unchanged. Hence this bound
extends to every state and Q=y_0-1<=H. All limiting or long-word claims
used here are actual finite inequalities with constants independent
of the word.

## 6. Finite local functions and the required proximal bounds

For a selected local checkpoint b>=1+epsilon^(-2), set

    A_loc=epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon,
    M=P+max{epsilon b,A_loc}, sigma=1/M,
    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b), Y=sigma Q.

M>0, sigma>0, U>=0, Y>=0. Let V_fin consist of0, v(0), and all
v_j of the block, and let

    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.

Every sampled v_j belongs to conv(V_fin), which is contained in K.
The global normal inequalities in Section3 therefore show that v_j
is still the projection of r_j onto this finite hull. At

    x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j, z_j=x_j-sigma v_j,

the plane with slope sigma v_j is active: its value is at least
every other plane value by the projection inequality. Thus
sigma v_j is a subgradient of phi at z_j, proving the proximal
relation and the local gradient of env_1 phi at x_j.

For any nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin,

    <v,-z_circ>
      =-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]
      <=-p+sigma p(P+A_loc)<=0.

Since p,q>=0, all its plane values remain nonpositive if the first
coordinate decreases from0 and the second increases from0. Including
the zero plane proves phi=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}.
The other zero region follows from the exact identity

    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0.

Indeed q<=p/epsilon and b>=1+epsilon^(-2). Equality at the threshold
is allowed. Increasing y above Y only reduces the expression, so
phi(U,y)=0 for every y>=Y.

The ordinary path starts at(1,0), ends at
(U+sigma epsilon b,Y), and has gradient sigma(epsilon,-1) there.
The physical checkpoint b gives (U,Y+sigma b).
For globalizing a maximum, positions alone are insufficient: we need
the proximal coordinates. They satisfy

    (z_j)_1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0,
    (z_j)_2=sigma[1+Q-y_j+q_j] in[0,sigma(1+Q)].

For a noncapped state,
u_j-p_j=R_sp(theta_j)t_j(cos theta_j-kappa sin theta_j)>=0.
For a capped state its normal representation gives u_j>=c=p_j.
Also 1<=y_j<=1+Q and0<=q_j<=1, proving the second line. These
bounds include the checkpoint query itself. They are the buffer used
by the max-envelope construction, and explain its b-1 transfer.

## 7. The one static max-envelope function

Partition the prescribed physical word, without reordering, into
(block_0,b_0,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k); let s_i be the ordinary
block sums. For i<k use the preceding construction on the actual steps
of block_i. Define

    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i),
    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i),
    delta_tail=D_k/(1+2s_k),
    Phi_k(z)=delta_tail max{z_k-ell_k,0},
    J(z)=max_(0<=i<=k) Phi_i(z),
    f(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].

All D_i and delta_tail are positive, since b_i-1>=epsilon^(-2)>0.
All ell_i are nonnegative. This fixes one finite maximum of affine
planes before the run; it does not splice functions after queries.
Each Phi_i is nonnegative and vanishes at0 by its pre-activation zero
region; the hinge does too. Hence J>=0 and J(0)=0.

Because J is finite, continuous, convex and nonnegative, the quadratic
in its envelope is coercive and strictly convex. It has a unique
minimizer z at each x. The minimizing equation is g=x-z in partial J(z).
For x',z',g' the two subgradient inequalities imply

    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.

For an arbitrary increment w, comparison with the trial proximal point z
gives the upper bound
f(x+w)<=f(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. Subgradient support at z, followed by
expansion at the actual proximal point z' for x+w, gives

    f(x+w)>=f(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.

These inequalities imply differentiability with gradient g and a
global supporting hyperplane at each x, hence convexity. The displayed
monotonicity inequality and Cauchy--Schwarz give1-Lipschitz gradient.
The minimum is attained and is0 because f>=0 and f(0)=0.

## 8. Global trajectory, terminal, and every ordered row

At the start of block_i the proposed global position has frozen
coordinates ell_j+D_j U_j for j<i, current coordinate ell_i+D_i,
and all later coordinates0. This is e_0 when i=0.
Use the local path in the current pair of coordinates, with the
other coordinates fixed. At every candidate global proximal point:

* The current Phi_i has the prescribed subgradient and nonnegative value.
* If i>=1, Phi_(i-1) has first input U_(i-1). The current proximal
  coordinate is at least ell_i+D_i U_i>=ell_i, so its second input is
  at least ell_i/D_(i-1)=sigma_(i-1)(1+Q_(i-1))>=Y_(i-1).
* For j<=i-2, the first input of Phi_j remains U_j. Its frozen second
  coordinate is ell_(j+1)+D_(j+1)U_(j+1)>=ell_(j+1)>=D_j Y_j.
* If i+1<k, Phi_(i+1) has first input at most0 because the current
  second proximal coordinate is <=ell_(i+1); its other input is0.
* Every more distant future pair has first input -ell_j/D_j<=0 and
  second input0. The terminal hinge satisfies the same inactivity
  comparison when it is a future component.

Thus every component except possibly the current one is0 at that
proximal point. The current component dominates and its subgradient
is a subgradient of J, also when several components tie at0. This
proves the actual global gradient at every ordinary and checkpoint
query. No old or future plane has been removed.
After the checkpoint the active first coordinate freezes at
ell_i+D_i U_i; the next coordinate is

    D_i(Y_i+sigma_i b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_i sigma_i(b_i-1)
                       =ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).

This closes induction using exactly the original chronological word.

In the terminal block, let u be the sum of already applied tail steps,
so0<=u<=s_k. The last position is ell_k+D_k-u delta_tail. Its proposed
proximal position is at least ell_k because

    D_k-(u+1)delta_tail
       >=D_k-(s_k+1)D_k/(1+2s_k)>=0.

The earlier components are in their zero regions there. Thus the gradient
is delta_tail e_k at every tail query and at the unqueried terminal.
At equality in the proximal comparison, delta_tail is a permitted
subgradient of the hinge. Evaluation gives exactly

    f(x_n)=delta_tail D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta_tail^2
          =D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].

For any two samples i,j of this actual function, including the terminal,
write z_i=x_i-g_i. Since g_i is a subgradient of J at z_i and
f_i=J(z_i)+||g_i||^2/2, expansion with the convention of
fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_ij=J(z_i)-J(z_j)+<g_i,z_j-z_i><=0.

This is one identity for EVERY ordered pair. A minimizer is also a
valid sample, with g_star=0 and J(z_star)=0. In particular both
directions of every optimum, prefix, remote, and terminal row hold.
Zero steps give repeated actual positions and gradients but are not
deleted. The full Gram matrix is formed from the displayed actual
vectors in R^(k+1), so is positive semidefinite without a support or
rank assumption on formal data.

## 9. The exact closest minimizer and payoffs(4),(5)

For k>=1 define

    x_star=(1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0), sigma_0(1+Q_0),0,...,0).

The first local pair is exactly at its center z_circ, so all its
planes have value0. The next first input is0 because its coordinate
equals ell_1; any later first input is nonpositive and its second
input0. The terminal hinge also vanishes. Therefore J(x_star)=0.
Since both terms in the envelope are nonnegative and the minimum is
attained, argmin f={J=0}: zero envelope value forces z=x and J(x)=0.

Every first-component slope is active at x_star. The exact identity

    e_0-x_star
      =[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/c] sigma_0(c,0,0,...)
        +(1+Q_0)sigma_0(epsilon,-1,0,...)

has nonnegative coefficients because P_0>=epsilon Q_0. If z belongs
to {J=0}, each active first-component plane gives a nonpositive inner
product of its slope with z-x_star. Thus
<e_0-x_star,z-x_star><=0 for every minimizer z. Expanding distance,

    ||e_0-z||^2=||e_0-x_star||^2+||z-x_star||^2
                 -2<e_0-x_star,z-x_star>
              >=||e_0-x_star||^2.

This proves that the displayed x_star is the closest minimizer of this
same function, uniquely as a projection, and that its squared radius is

    sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2]>0.

Combining with the terminal value and D_k recursion cancels exactly
sigma_0^2 and yields the frozen true-distance bound

    W_n(h)>=(b_0-1)^2/
       ([(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k))
       * product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i]^2.                 (A4)

For the mass-only bound, epsilon<=1 and nonnegativity imply

    sqrt((epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2)
       <=P_0+epsilon+(1+Q_0)/epsilon<=M_0,
    M_i<=c s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A}.

These are in the correct directions for a lower bound on the payoff.
They prove

    W_n(h)>=1/(1+2s_k)
       * product_(i=0)^(k-1)
           [(b_i-1)/(c s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A})]^2.           (A5)

The numerator is b_i-1 throughout. For k=0 the separate one-dimensional
function env_1(delta max{z,0}), delta=1/(1+2s_0), starts at1, has
closest minimizer0, keeps gradient delta through the full tail, and has
2f(x_n)=1/(1+2s_0). Multiplying any of these functions by L gives the
required physical h_t/L update and the same normalized payoff.

## 10. The distinct source-format realization

Here the parameters and curve are unchanged, but the function and
activation levels are intentionally different. Use any a_sp>=2A/c.
In each local block apply the preceding construction to alpha=h/2,
with local sum S=s_i/2, local checkpoint b=b_i/2, local allowance
A_half=c a_sp/2>=A, and

    sigma=1/(c S+epsilon b+A_half).

The physical condition b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)) gives the required
local threshold. Since P<=cS and A_loc<=A,
sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1 and sigma(P+A_loc)<=1. These are exactly the
two scale inequalities used for the zero regions. Thus the local
envelope f_i=env_1 phi_i has all previously proved local properties,
with corresponding U_i,Y_i. Its unbuffered local position transfer is

    G_i=sigma b=b_i/[c(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i].

Define new numbers D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,
D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i, ell'_(i+1)=D'_i Y_i, and

    F(x)=(1/2) sum_(i<k) (D'_i)^2
              f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
          +(1/2) H_delta(x_k-ell'_k),
    delta=D'_k/(1+s_k),
    H_delta(z)=0 if z<=0;
               z^2/2 if0<=z<=delta;
               delta z-delta^2/2 if z>=delta.

Each rescaled pair term is convex and1-smooth on its two coordinates.
The hinge envelope H_delta is convex and1-smooth in one coordinate.
Every coordinate occurs in at most two terms. Summing their quadratic
upper bounds with weight1/2 gives remainder at most ||w||^2/2.
To explicitly infer gradient Lipschitzness, apply this upper bound
to F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x, with increment -(grad F(x)-grad F(y)).
Convexity makes y a global minimizer of the tilted function, giving

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
       >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.

The inequality with x,y exchanged gives the gradient contraction after
addition and Cauchy--Schwarz. It also proves all ordered interpolation
rows for F, including its terminal and the minimizer0.

Every term is nonnegative and vanishes at0, so min F=F(0)=0. Along
the actual local positions the current first coordinate remains at
least ell'_i because U_i>=0, and the current second coordinate is
between0 and D'_i Y_i until the checkpoint. Earlier terms remain in
their post-checkpoint zero region, and later terms remain in their
pre-activation zero region. Differentiability implies that each zero
term has zero gradient. Thus only the current term contributes, and
its factor1/2 converts every physical h_t into the chosen local h_t/2.
The checkpoint places the next coordinate at
ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). All samples, including zeros, keep their original
physical index. There is no restarted subproblem clock.

In the final block the gradient is delta/2; at cumulative tail sum u
the last shifted position is D'_k-u delta/2. It stays at least delta,
since u<=s_k and D'_k=delta(1+s_k). Therefore

    F(x_n)=(1/2)[delta(D'_k-s_k delta/2)-delta^2/2]
          =(D'_k)^2/[4(1+s_k)].

Initial e_0 and the specified genuine minimizer0 have distance1.
It follows that

    W_n(h)>=1/[2(1+s_k)]
          *product_(i<k)[b_i/(c(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i)]^2.  (A5a)

For k=0 use just the last Huber term and D'_0=1. This construction
proves exactly the source-format corollary with a_sp=2A/c allowed.
It does not assert that0 is the closest minimizer for k>=1, and it
does not borrow the radius from the different max-envelope function.

## 11. Boundary-case checks

For an empty ordinary block, m=0, r_0=v_0=(epsilon,-1) and P=Q=0.
All plane, scale, normal and zero-region inequalities above remain
valid. Adding any number of zero ordinary steps leaves these values
and the actual positions unchanged while retaining their samples.
The empty terminal block has s_k=0: in the max-envelope function
delta_tail=D_k and its terminal proximal point is exactly at the
hinge boundary; in the half-sum function delta=D'_k and the terminal
position is at the Huber boundary. Both displayed terminal values hold.

For k=1 the product after the first factor in(A4) is empty. If both
ordinary blocks are empty, it reads (b_0-1)^2/(1+epsilon^2), with the
radius and function already exhibited above. There is no ell_2 or
missing middle block to define. For k=0, including n=0, the scalar
Huber calculations give the stated formulas; (A5) is1 at n=0 while
the separately weaker(A5a) is1/2.

At b=1+epsilon^(-2), every post-checkpoint plane comparison is weakly
nonpositive, including equality for the endpoint plane. Max ties and
Huber boundary subgradients are permitted by the preceding proofs.
No step requires strictness. Large selected b only enlarges the scale
denominator when epsilon b dominates. It preserves U>=0 and all zero
regions; the epsilon b contribution is never discarded. Large ordinary
steps are handled by the cap branch. Large terminal steps are handled
by choosing the tail slope using the entire nonnegative sum. Thus no
finite stepsize is excluded beyond the selected checkpoint threshold.

## 12. Matched certified constants

Set epsilon=kappa and let kappa decrease to0. Then
c^2=(1+kappa^2)exp(2kappa Theta). The matching parameter

    gamma_match=(c^2-1-kappa^2)/2
               =(1+kappa^2)(exp(2kappa Theta)-1)/2

is positive for every permitted kappa and satisfies
gamma_match/kappa->pi/2. The circular c_Y=sqrt(1+2gamma_match+kappa^2)
is EXACTLY c, and R_Y=sqrt(kappa^2+(1+gamma_match)^2)->1.
Write base_Y=c(1+gamma_match+R_Y)/[kappa(gamma_match+R_Y)].
The source constant is H_Y=base_Y^(R_Y/gamma_match)-1, and
base_Y is asymptotic to2/kappa.
Consequently

    kappa log(H_Y+1)/log(1/kappa)->2/pi.

On the spiral side,
T~exp(d Theta/kappa)/d and
A~c(1+kappa)T/kappa. The logarithms of the remaining factors,
multiplied by kappa, tend to0. Hence

    kappa log A->pi/2,
    kappa log(2A/c)->pi/2.

Since C_Y=kappa+(H_Y+1)/kappa, its logarithm has the larger
log(1/kappa)/kappa scale. Thus A/C_Y->0 and the ratio of the
displayed sufficient choices (2A/c)/(2C_Y/c) tends0. Adding the
source's polynomial floors2 and2B_0^Y+2 to both choices does not change
these limits. These are comparisons of certified auxiliary constants
at the same epsilon and c, not claims that either construction's
optimal possible auxiliary constant has been determined. They imply
no uniform o(1) relative error against B_n.

## 13. Actual finite equal-angle words and their total cost

Fix the permitted parameters and any integer M>=1. Put
Delta=Theta/M and theta_l=Theta-l Delta for0<=l<=M. Define

    A_D=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa,
    t_0=0, t_l=B_D+A_D t_(l-1),
    r_l=R_sp(theta_l)[(1+t_l)e(theta_l)+kappa t_l e_t(theta_l)],
    a_D=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D,
    alpha_l=a_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D t_(l-1).

Every coefficient A_D,B_D is nonnegative and every alpha_l is
positive: 1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta>=0 and B_D>0.
Expanding r_(l-1)/R_sp(theta_l) in the orthonormal basis at theta_l gives tangent
component
exp(-kappa Delta)[(1+t_(l-1))sin Delta+kappa t_(l-1)cos Delta],
which equals kappa t_l. Its radial component is
exp(-kappa Delta)[(1+t_(l-1))cos Delta-kappa t_(l-1)sin Delta].
Subtracting it from1+t_l gives exactly alpha_l. Thus

    r_l-r_(l-1)=alpha_l v(theta_l).

Also r_l-v(theta_l)=R_sp(theta_l)t_l n_sp(theta_l) is a valid
supporting normal. Therefore v(theta_l)=Pi_K(r_l). The finite
chronological word (alpha_M,...,alpha_1), starting at r_M, follows
these actual states to r_0=(epsilon,-1). At theta_M=0 its last
backward step is precisely the allowed cap-boundary equality.

Let S_M=sum_(l=1)^M alpha_l,
P_M=(r_M)_1-epsilon, Q_M=-(r_M)_2-1. Then

    P_M=c(1+t_M)-epsilon, 1+Q_M=c kappa t_M.

For large enough M, A_D>1 because A_D'(0)=d/kappa>0. Discrete
telescoping t_l-t_(l-1)=B_D+(A_D-1)t_(l-1) yields

    t_M=B_D(A_D^M-1)/(A_D-1),
    S_M=M a_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D(t_M-M B_D)/(A_D-1).

The required limits are
A_D^M->exp(d Theta/kappa), B_D/(A_D-1)->1/d,
M B_D->Theta/kappa, and M a_D->(kappa+1/kappa)Theta.
Consequently

    t_M->T,
    S_M->(1+kappa^2)T/d-kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d.

These limits are taken only after establishing each exact finite word
and its cost. No continuous-time proxy or arithmetic sweep is used.

## 14. Paid activation excess and the exact original failing row

For the same local support construction, its endpoint plane evaluated
at0 is

    sigma <(epsilon,-1),-z_circ>
      =sigma^2 epsilon
         [epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon-sigma^(-1)].

Thus phi(0)=0 necessarily requires
sigma^(-1)>=epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon. This is also the exact original
optimum interpolation obstruction: at the end of the ordinary block,
r_m=v_m=(epsilon,-1), so the local proximal point is z_circ, its
proximal value is0, its gradient is g=sigma(epsilon,-1), its position
is x_end=z_circ+g, and its smooth value is ||g||^2/2. Pairing these
with the proposed optimum jet (0,0,0) gives

    Q_(end,star)=-<g,z_circ>
      =sigma^2 epsilon
         [epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon-sigma^(-1)].              (AQ)

This identity is in the original physical variables and the orientation
of fact1252b8823bb5a290. A positive value invalidates the proposed
common zero/minimum normalization; it does not make the local envelope
nonconvex or deny it a minimizer somewhere else.

For the equal-angle family define the excess after the full horizontal
allowance has been paid,

    E_M=epsilon+P_M+(1+Q_M)/epsilon-c S_M.

The exact endpoint and total-cost limits give

    E_M->E_sp
      =c[1+(kappa/epsilon-2kappa^2/d)T
                 +kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d].

On the diagonal epsilon=kappa<=1/2, this is

    E_sp=c[1+(1-3kappa^2)T/d+kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d]>0.

Here 1-3kappa^2>=1/4>0. The exponentially growing term T dominates
as kappa->0, proving

    kappa log E_sp->pi/2,
    kappa A/E_sp->1.

For each fixed kappa, E_M approaches E_sp along actual finite legal
words. If a universal additive allowance A_trial is proposed with
sigma^(-1)=c S_M+A_trial, equation(AQ) becomes

    Q_(end,star)=sigma^2 epsilon(E_M-A_trial).

Thus A_trial<E_sp fails on some finite member, and a universal
allowance must be at least E_sp. If the proposed denominator also
retains epsilon b, the row is instead
sigma^2 epsilon(E_M-A_trial-epsilon b). On epsilon=kappa take the
legal fixed local checkpoint b=1+kappa^(-2); a universal allowance
must then satisfy A_trial>=E_sp-kappa b. The subtracted quantity is
kappa+1/kappa, polynomial, so it does not change the positive
exponential asymptotic scale. For the source-format physical word
the same test uses its doubled physical checkpoint and doubled
ordinary steps, because these expressions are local-step quantities.

This closes only a polynomial additive activation allowance within
this same support curve, endpoint and zero-height reset interface.
The endpoint optimum row already forces it, independently of remote
row tests. It says nothing against positive-height gluing, a different
endpoint or curve, or a better unrestricted adversary. It gives no
upper bound on W_n for these finite words. The horizontal slack cS-P
has been paid before claiming the obstruction.

## 15. Finite-word uniformity and the remaining exact-profile gap

For fixed permitted epsilon,kappa, the whole construction works for
every finite nonnegative word and every admissible checkpoint subset.
The constants c,H,A are independent of its length, sums, and maximum
step. This is the finite-word uniformity that survived the audit.

It does not remove the following losses in(A5): the checkpoint
threshold, b_i-1 instead of b_i, c>1 on every ordinary sum,
max{epsilon b_i,A}, and the exact terminal1+2s_k. If all s_i>0 for
i<k, the diagnostic expression

    I=product_(i<k)(b_i/s_i)^2/(1+2s_k)

has logarithmic ratio to(A5)'s right side equal to

    Loss=2sum_i log[c+max{epsilon b_i,A}/s_i]
           -2sum_i log(1-1/b_i).

It is >=2k log c. The inequalities log(1+x)<=x for x>=0 and
-log(1-u)<=u/(1-u) for0<=u<1 give exactly

    Loss<=2k(c-1)+2sum_i max{epsilon b_i,A}/s_i
                         +2sum_i 1/(b_i-1).

For(A5a), relative to the analogous product with denominator s_i and
the same terminal1/[2(1+s_k)], the exact logarithmic loss is

    Loss_source=2sum_i log[c+(c a_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i].

There is no identification of either diagnostic expression with B_n.
Zero ordinary sums make these diagnostics unavailable but do not
make the actual bounds undefined. Large epsilon b_i/s_i, the number
of factors, and the activation allowance are retained in all claims.
Pointwise parameter improvements are insufficient to control these
quantities uniformly over words and growing numbers of checkpoints.

The unresolved original affirmative target is the quantifier statement

    for every delta>0 there exists N(delta) such that
    for every n>=N(delta) and every h in[0,infinity)^n,
                     W_n(h)>=(1-delta)/B_n.

A sufficient next theorem for this restricted channel would replace
W_n(h) on the left by the supremum of(A4) over its allowed parameters
and checkpoint selections, including the Huber empty selection. The
supremum is finite, since each bound is realized by a legal instance
and W_n(h) is finite for fixed h. Neither that sufficient comparison
nor its negation has been established by this audit. A finite
checkpoint set can be maximized at fixed parameters; continuous
parameter optimization should be expressed as a supremum unless
attainment is separately proved.

The surviving result is precisely the finite-word construction in
Sections3--11, the matched certified-constant comparison in Section12,
and the finite paid obstruction in Sections13--14. The next asymptotic
comparison remains unproved. No formal acceptance, original-target
completion, or authorization for another round is inferred.

# A static smooth-convex witness above the benchmark on the original ruler

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL CANDIDATE; UNVERIFIED.
Worker max4, round58. Assignment TASK.md; steering60da2cf01388bce5.
This is a prescribed-word lower witness, not a minimax resolution.

The only accepted mathematical input is fact04219554e41d9750, restricted
to its ORIGINAL benchmark ray and certified rational enclosure. The two
unverified source drafts c8fcf4ced5a34046 and8880a32b8502cf2f are provenance,
not predecessors: all geometry and scalar arguments needed here are
derived together below. No mathematical program, numerical evaluation,
parameter scan, executed recurrence, solver, formal gate, installation,
or sub-agent is used. Source originals are preserved.

## 1. Definitions and quantities to be realized

Set lambda=1+sqrt(2). For each integer m>=6 use exactly

    beta=1-1/m, c=lambda*(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, b_j=1+a*c^j.

Every child retains the SAME enclosing m. Only tau=1 is used anywhere
in this theorem. The finite lower bound below is proved whenever the
selected set is nonempty; this holds for every sufficiently large m.

Retain the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark:

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2,
    G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8*x*y)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every split index is an integer. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 gives

    Z_1=lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m >0,
    lambda^(-r)*B_(2^r-1) <= Z_1
      <=lambda^(-r)*(sqrt(B_(2^r))+3/100)^2, r>=0.       (1)

Define the EXACT geometric parameters and costs

    K=14/5, E=1/100, kappa=K/m, epsilon=E/m,
    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    chi=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp), d=1-kappa^2,
    T=(exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d,
    H=chi*[1+(1+kappa)*T]-1,
    A=epsilon+(H+1)/epsilon.                           (2)

Here 0<kappa<1 and 0<epsilon<=1/2. The symbol H without a subscript
denotes the geometric vertical allowance, not a physical word H_j.
Use the frozen selection rule, without altering any finite threshold:

    R_cut=max{A/epsilon,1+2/epsilon^2},
    J=max{1,ceil(log(R_cut/a)/log c)}.                  (3)

If J>=m the selection is empty and k=0. Otherwise select every FIRST
occurrence of the levels J,J+1,...,m-1 and no other physical step, and
put k=m-J.
Checkpoint i, 0<=i<k, has level J+i and value b^(i)=b_(J+i).
Its incoming ordinary word is denoted by Aword_i and its sum by s_i.
The terminal ordinary word is Aword_k, with sum s_k. In the empty case
Aword_0=H_m and s_0=S_m.

For the nonempty case define the scalar value to be realized:

    P_m = 1/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]
          * product_(i=0)^(k-1)
              [b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i))]^2.   (4)

For the empty case define P_m=1/[2*(1+S_m)].

Write q_rc=8+4sqrt(2) and theta=5*pi/(28*log lambda), and define

    L_joint=Z_1/(2*lambda)
         *exp[-1+(q_rc-14*pi/5-1/50)*(1-theta)].        (5)

The original normalized worst-case error is

    W_n(h)=sup 2*(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2), (6)

over all finite Euclidean dimensions, convex differentiable functions
with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum x_star, and x_0!=x_star,
under actual GD x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t). The final theorem appears
after its supporting construction and estimates. It concerns only this
prescribed family; the original minimax and uniform all-word target
remain open.

## 2. Original physical indices, sums and the paid threshold

Put zeta=a/(c-2)=beta/[c*(c-2)] and v_mass=c*(c-2). For x=1/m,

    v_mass=1+(4+2sqrt(2))*x+lambda^2*x^2>1>beta>0,
    0<zeta<1,
    S_j=zeta*c^j+(1-zeta)*2^j-1, 0<=j<=m.            (7)

Indeed S_0=0. Substitution into S_(j+1)=2*S_j+1+a*c^j gives the
next identity because zeta*(c-2)=a. This proves (7) by induction.

Index physical steps from0. The first occurrence of b_j is at
t_j=2^j-1. The prefix before it is H_j; for j<m-1 the word between
t_j and t_(j+1) is H_j; after t_(m-1) is H_(m-1). These statements follow inductively
from the displayed concatenation, preserving its fixed enclosing m.
Thus for J<m the decomposition is exactly

    H_J, b_J, H_J, b_(J+1), H_(J+1), b_(J+2), ...,
                    H_(m-2), b_(m-1), H_(m-1).        (8)

In particular s_0=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-1) for1<=i<k, and s_k=S_(m-1).
There are k original selected steps and n_m-k ordinary steps. All
unselected copies remain in their actual physical positions.

From (3), a*c^J>=R_cut. Therefore EVERY selected b^(i) satisfies

    b^(i)>=2*(1+epsilon^(-2)),
    epsilon*b^(i)>=epsilon+A>A.                       (9)

The doubled source-format threshold is met at finite m, not merely
asymptotically. It also implies the smaller one-envelope threshold.

## 3. Independent-parameter arc and finite backward trajectories

We prove the local construction for arbitrary0<epsilon<=1/2 and
0<kappa<1, with the exact costs (2). No ordering kappa<=epsilon is used.
For0<=u<=theta_sp define

    e(u)=(cos u,-sin u), t(u)=(-sin u,-cos u),
    R(u)=chi*exp(-kappa*u),
    v(u)=R(u)*e(u)=(p(u),-q(u)), n(u)=e(u)+kappa*t(u).

Let K_arc be the convex hull of0 and this compact arc. Its endpoints
are v(0)=(chi,0) and v(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1). Differentiation gives
p'=-R*(kappa*cos u+sin u)<0 and q'=R*(cos u-kappa*sin u). Therefore

    epsilon<=p(u)<=chi, q(u)>=0, q(u)/p(u)<=1/epsilon.  (10)

In particular q is allowed to exceed1. For w-u=Delta,

    <n(u),v(w)>/R(u)
       =exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos Delta+kappa*sin Delta).

The derivative in Delta is
-(1+kappa^2)*exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin Delta. On the entire possible
interval [-theta_sp,theta_sp], the expression has maximum1 at0. Also
<n(u),0>=0<R(u). Thus n(u) supports K_arc at v(u). At v(0), the
additional vector (0,1) supports K_arc because q>=0.

Take ANY finite ordered list alpha_1,...,alpha_N>=0. Define backward
vectors r_j and selected arc points v_j as follows. Start with
r_N=v_N=(epsilon,-1) and angle u_N=theta_sp. Suppose r_j=(U_j,-Y_j)
is defined. With alpha=alpha_j, if

    Y_j<=kappa*[U_j+(alpha-1)*chi],                   (11)

set v_(j-1)=v(0), u_(j-1)=0, r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha*v(0).
Otherwise use the unique u in(0,u_j] satisfying

    <r_j,e(u)-t(u)/kappa>/R(u)=1-alpha,              (12)

and set u_(j-1)=u, v_(j-1)=v(u), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha*v(u).
These are exact definitions, not executed root searches.

Here is existence, uniqueness and the projection proof. Inductively

    U_j>=epsilon, Y_j>=1, Y_j/U_j<=1/epsilon;           (13)

at every uncapped point there is a_j>=0 with

    r_j=R(u_j)*[(1+a_j)*e(u_j)+kappa*a_j*t(u_j)].       (14)

Initially a_N=0. In (11) let mu=U_j+(alpha-1)*chi. Then mu>0 and
r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu*n(0)+(0,kappa*mu-Y_j). Both are nonnegative
supporting-normal combinations, proving projection to v(0). The
inequality becomes Y_(j-1)<=kappa*(U_(j-1)-chi), so all earlier
backward entries are capped too.

If (11) fails, the point is not already capped. The left side of
(12) is1 at u_j by (14), less than1-alpha at0, and has derivative

    (kappa+kappa^(-1))*(U_j*cos u+Y_j*sin u)/R(u)>0.

This gives exactly one root, including alpha=0 with root u_j.
Representation (14) shows that the polar angle of r_j is at least
u_j, so <r_j,t(u)>=0 at that root. Equation (12) says that
r_j+(alpha-1)*v(u) is a nonnegative multiple of n(u): its t-component
is kappa times its e-component. This proves (14) for j-1 and the
projection property. Adding alpha*(p,-q) preserves (13) by (10).
Finally, if x-v has nonpositive dot product with every w-v in K_arc,
then ||x-w||^2>=||x-v||^2; thus all the supporting-normal arguments
indeed prove v_j=Pi_(K_arc)(r_j).

Define

    P=sum_(j=1)^N alpha_j*p(u_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_(j=1)^N alpha_j*q(u_(j-1)), S=sum_j alpha_j.

Then r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), 0<=P<=chi*S and P>=epsilon*Q.
To bound Q independently of the word, consider two consecutive
uncapped angles u_old<=u_new and Delta=u_new-u_old. Taking the
t(u_old) component of r_old=r_new+alpha*v(u_old) gives exactly

    a_old=B_Delta+A_Delta*a_new,
    A_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin Delta/kappa.          (15)

Both coefficients are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2>0,

    A_Delta'-(d/kappa)*A_Delta
       =-(1+kappa^(-2))*exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin Delta<=0.

Hence A_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa), while B_Delta<=Delta/kappa and
1+d*B_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa). It follows that
1+d*a_old<=exp(d*Delta/kappa)*(1+d*a_new). Telescoping from a_N=0
gives a_j<=T. By (14),

    Y_j=R(u_j)*[(1+a_j)*sin u_j+kappa*a_j*cos u_j]
       <=chi*[1+(1+kappa)*T]=1+H.

A cap leaves Y unchanged. Consequently

    0<=Q<=H, 0<=P<=chi*S, P>=epsilon*Q.               (16)

The needed geometry has now been proved without the false restriction
q<=1, without nonnegative horizontal normals, and without any per-step
unpriced error. The half-sum assembly below uses physical zero regions;
it does not require bounds on horizontal proximal coordinates.

## 4. Finite local functions and their zero regions

For a physical ordinary block of sum s and its physical checkpoint b,
apply Section3 to its entries divided by2. Write N for its length and
P,Q,r_j,v_j for that HALF-step construction. Set b_loc=b/2 and

    sigma=1/(chi*s/2+epsilon*b/2+A),
    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma*(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
    U=1-sigma*(P+epsilon*b_loc), Y=sigma*Q,
    V_fin={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_N},
    phi(z)=sigma*max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>,
    f_loc(x)=min_z [phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2].              (17)

By (16), P<=chi*s/2 and Q<=H. Thus U>=0 and

    sigma*[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.               (18)

The function phi is a finite nonnegative convex maximum of affine
functions. The minimizing objective defining f_loc is coercive and
strictly convex, so its proximal point z exists uniquely. If g=x-z,
the minimizing condition is g in partial phi(z). To justify both
directions directly, this subgradient inequality makes the cost at
any other w at least its cost at z plus ||w-z||^2/2. Conversely,
optimality at z, tested against z+t*(w-z) for0<t<1, and convexity of
phi imply phi(w)-phi(z)>=<g,w-z>-t*||w-z||^2/2. Letting t decrease
to0 proves the subgradient inequality. For two such pairs,
the two subgradient inequalities imply
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. Moreover using z as trial point at x+w gives

    f_loc(x+w)<=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2.

Using the actual new proximal point z' and the subgradient inequality
at z gives

    f_loc(x+w)>=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.

These bounds prove differentiability with gradient g, global convexity,
and a1-Lipschitz gradient. Also f_loc>=0 and {f_loc=0}={phi=0}.

At x_j=z_circ+sigma*r_j, put z_j=x_j-sigma*v_j. Since the convex hull
of V_fin is contained in K_arc and includes v_j, its projection
inequality implies that sigma*v_j is an active subgradient of phi at
z_j. Thus grad f_loc(x_j)=sigma*v_j. In particular the original
ordered half-steps run exactly from x_0=(1,0) to
(U+sigma*epsilon*b_loc,Y); the step b_loc then reaches
(U,Y+sigma*b_loc).

For every nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin, (10) and (18) give

    <v,-z_circ>
      =-p+sigma*[p*(epsilon+P)+q*(1+Q)]<=0.

Therefore phi=f_loc=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}. Also b_loc>=1+epsilon^(-2)
by (9), so

    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma*[(1-b_loc)*epsilon*p+q]<=0.

Increasing the second coordinate decreases this inner product. Hence

    f_loc(U,y)=0 for every y>=Y.                      (19)

Along the actual local path the first coordinate decreases and the
second increases. Before its checkpoint they obey x_1>=U and
0<=x_2<=Y. These are physical coordinate statements, valid independently
of the sign of the supporting normal. Zero steps, if present, retain
their original index and cause no exception to the construction.

## 5. One finite-dimensional static function and every physical update

First suppose J<m. Apply Section4 separately in advance to each actual
incoming block Aword_i and its checkpoint b^(i), i=0,...,k-1. All its symbols receive
the subscript i: sigma_i,P_i,Q_i,phi_i,f_i,U_i,Y_i,z_circ,i.
Define positive scales and nonnegative offsets by

    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    G_i=sigma_i*b^(i)/2
       =b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i)),
    D_(i+1)=D_i*G_i,
    ell_(i+1)=D_i*Y_i,
    delta_tail=D_k/(1+s_k).                           (20)

The basis e_0,...,e_k is the standard orthonormal basis of R^(k+1).
For any delta>0 define the one-sided Huber function

    Hub_delta(z)=0                     if z<=0,
                 z^2/2                 if0<=z<=delta,
                 delta*z-delta^2/2      if z>=delta.

Its continuous derivative is max{0,min{z,delta}}, which is nondecreasing
and1-Lipschitz. The ACTUAL function used in the theorem is

    F_m(x)=(1/2)*sum_(i=0)^(k-1) D_i^2
                    *f_i((x_i-ell_i)/D_i,x_(i+1)/D_i)
           +(1/2)*Hub_(delta_tail)(x_k-ell_k).         (21)

This is a single function fixed before running GD. The finite lists
of planes, implicit backward roots, scales and shifts are mathematical
definitions determined by the entire prescribed word. No trajectory
is replayed or numerically solved to define (21). Its dimension is
exactly k+1=m-J+1 in this nonempty case, finite at every m. Each local
maximum has only finitely many planes even though the auxiliary arc is continuous.

The function is nonnegative and convex. Every ell_i>=0, so at x=0
each local input is in {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}, and the terminal term vanishes.
Therefore F_m(0)=min F_m=0. Each scaled local term before its factor1/2
has the1-smooth upper inequality on its two coordinates. Summing,

    F_m(x+w)<=F_m(x)+<grad F_m(x),w>
          +(1/4)*[sum_(i<k)(w_i^2+w_(i+1)^2)+w_k^2]
       <=F_m(x)+<grad F_m(x),w>+||w||^2/2.             (22)

Every coordinate is counted at most twice. This pays the overlap cost
once in the explicit coefficient1/2; no dimension factor remains.
For completeness, (22) implies1-Lipschitz gradient here: fix y and
apply (22) to V(x)=F_m(x)-<grad F_m(y),x> with w=-grad V(x).
Convexity makes y a minimizer of V. Consequently

    F_m(x)-F_m(y)-<grad F_m(y),x-y>
       >=||grad F_m(x)-grad F_m(y)||^2/2.              (23)

Add the reversed inequality and apply Cauchy--Schwarz to obtain the
Lipschitz bound. This argument does not assume a least smoothness
constant or an extra rescaling of the steps.

At the start of block i the claimed physical state is

    x_j=ell_j+D_j*U_j for j<i,
    x_i=ell_i+D_i,
    x_j=0 for j>i.                                    (24)

It is true initially at e_0. Follow the scaled local path in coordinates
i,i+1, leaving the other coordinates fixed. Before its checkpoint,

    x_i>=ell_i+D_i*U_i>=ell_i,
    0<=x_(i+1)<=D_i*Y_i=ell_(i+1).

For a previous component j<i, its first input is U_j and its second
is at least ell_(j+1)/D_j=Y_j. Thus (19) makes that component zero.
For a future component j>i, its first input is nonpositive and its
second input is0, so the other zero region makes it zero. The terminal
term is also zero until its activation. A differentiable nonnegative
function has zero gradient at any zero, since every directional
derivative there must vanish. Thus only the current component contributes.

Its global gradient is D_i/2 times the local gradient. A physical
step h_t therefore induces exactly the local step h_t/2. This proves
that the candidate local path is the actual GD path of (21) for every
ordinary step, in its given order. The physical checkpoint b^(i) then
leaves

    x_i=ell_i+D_i*U_i,
    x_(i+1)=D_i*(Y_i+sigma_i*b^(i)/2)
           =ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).

This proves (24) for the next block. No component is removed or changed
at a transition; earlier components remain zero by the same inequality.
All steps, including all unselected occurrences of large levels, have
been included. Empty blocks and zero steps require no deletion.

During the terminal block let u be the sum of its executed physical
steps. Since0<=u<=s_k, its shifted moving coordinate is

    x_k-ell_k=D_k-delta_tail*u/2
       >=D_k-delta_tail*s_k/2
        =delta_tail*(1+s_k/2)>=delta_tail.            (25)

Every earlier component remains zero. The actual gradient equals
(delta_tail/2)*e_k at every terminal query and at the terminal point.
Hence direct substitution into (21) gives

    F_m(x_(n_m))=D_k^2/[4*(1+s_k)].                   (26)

Equations (20), (26), s_k=S_(m-1), and ||e_0-0||=1 prove EXACTLY (4).
For arbitrary L>0 set f_m=L*F_m. It is L-smooth and GD with physical
steps h_t/L has the identical path, while the normalization cancels L.

For the literal empty-selection case J>=m use k=0,D_0=1,ell_0=0,
and (21) reduces to F_m=Hub_(1/(1+S_m))/2 on R, starting at1.
The same terminal calculation gives normalized payoff1/[2*(1+S_m)].
Thus the exact selection rule also has a defined realization at every
earlier m>=6. No empty-case asymptotic is used in the final theorem.

## 6. All original interpolation conditions and the actual radius

For every ordered pair a,b of the actual query points, the terminal
point, and ANY minimizer, write g_a=grad F_m(x_a), f_a=F_m(x_a).
Inequality (23) with x=x_b,y=x_a yields

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2<=0.     (27)

Thus every original ordered smooth-convex interpolation row holds,
including both orientations of optimum and terminal rows. It is a
global function inequality, so no untested nonlocal pair or cycle
remains. For f_m=L*F_m the final squared-gradient term is divided by
2L, as required. The update equations were proved in Section5. Any
Gram matrix of these points and gradients is positive semidefinite
because they are actual vectors in R^(k+1): its quadratic form is
the squared norm of their indicated linear combination. Zero steps
give equal actual jets but still retain their physical positions.

The exact minimizer used in (4) is0, already proved to minimize F_m;
its radius is exactly ||e_0||=1. The true closest radius is also
accounted for explicitly. Write z_circ,i=(c_(i,1),c_(i,2)). The zero
set of the local phi_i, and hence f_i, is exactly

    z_1<=c_(i,1),
    epsilon*(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.           (28)

The endpoint planes make these inequalities necessary. They suffice
because each other arc slope (p,-q) is

    [(p-epsilon*q)/chi]*(chi,0)+q*(epsilon,-1),

a nonnegative combination by (10). Since (21) is a sum of nonnegative
functions, its whole minimum set is the explicit nonempty polyhedron

    C_min={x: x_i<=zeta_i,
                  epsilon*x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon*zeta_i-D_i*c_(i,2)
                    for0<=i<k;
               x_k<=ell_k},
    zeta_i=ell_i+D_i*c_(i,1).                         (29)

It contains0. For k>=1 the initial point e_0 violates the first inequality,
because zeta_0=1-sigma_0*(epsilon+P_0)<1. It is therefore outside this
closed minimum set. For k=0 the set is the nonpositive half-line and
the same conclusion holds from initial1. Its exact closest radius is

    rho_m=dist(e_0,C_min), 0<rho_m<=1.                 (30)

Attainment follows by minimizing squared distance on the nonempty
closed set after restricting to a sufficiently large compact ball;
strict convexity makes the projection unique. The radius about the
specified minimizer is1, while the true normalized value is exactly
P_m/rho_m^2>=P_m. Thus using the closest minimizer strengthens the final
bound; there is no assumption that0 is the closest minimizer.

## 7. Distinguishing the two geometric formulas and their radius costs

Define, on precisely the same selected positions, the scalar reference

    P5_m=1/(1+2*s_k)*product_(i<k)
               [(b^(i)-1)/(chi*s_i+max{epsilon*b^(i),A})]^2. (31)

This is the geometry draft's main mass-only formula(7), and the scalar
draft's F5. In that different one-envelope realization it is a lower
estimate on a payoff normalized by its closest-minimizer radius. Its
finite factors have b-1, a maximum allowance and terminal1/(1+2*s_k).

The ACTUAL function in this document is instead the half-sum (21).
Its specified-minimizer payoff P_m is the geometry draft's formula(8),
equivalently the scalar draft's F5a, with a_sp=2*A/chi. Its factors
have b, the additive allowance2*A and terminal1/[2*(1+s_k)]. Its
specified radius1 and closest radius(30) are properties of this same
actual function, proved above. The one-envelope radius formula from
the other draft is not assigned to (21).

For the remaining calculation (31) is just an explicitly defined
scalar reference. No assertion about the other draft's realization
is used as a premise. In particular one must not replace the ACTUAL
finite value P_m by P5_m. Section9 proves their ratio tends1 with
all accumulated losses paid. The source-format construction itself
has already been derived jointly in Sections3--6.

## 8. The exact activation phase and its logarithmic correction

The cost A in (2) satisfies the useful exact identity

    A=epsilon+sqrt(1+epsilon^2)/[epsilon*(1-kappa)]
           *[exp(theta_sp/kappa)-kappa*exp(kappa*theta_sp)]. (32)

Indeed 1+(1+kappa)*T=(exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)-kappa)/(1-kappa),
and chi*exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(theta_sp/kappa).

With fixed K=14/5,E=1/100, the integral defining arctan gives
theta_sp=pi/2-E/m+O(m^(-3)). Factoring exp(theta_sp/kappa) from the bracket
in (32), its relative subtracted term is exponentially small in m.
The separate additive epsilon is also exponentially smaller than the
remaining positive term. The logarithms of sqrt(1+epsilon^2) and
1/(1-kappa) have bounded Taylor remainders of orders m^(-2) and m^(-1).
It follows, with constants independent of m, that

    log A=pi*m/(2*K)+log m-log E-E/K+O(1/m),
    log(A/epsilon)=pi*m/(2*K)+2*log m-2*log E-E/K+O(1/m). (33)

For explicit remainder justification, the arctan integral differs
from epsilon by at most epsilon^3/3; the log expansion follows from
integrating1/(1+t) on a fixed interval about0. After some fixed m,
theta_sp is bounded below by a positive constant, so the exponential
relative remainders are bounded by a fixed multiple of exp(-c_0*m)
for some c_0>0. Taking their logarithms is then justified by the
same integral bound. These estimates require no evaluated exponential.

The other quantity1+2/epsilon^2 in R_cut has polynomial growth, so
R_cut=A/epsilon for every sufficiently large m. Since
log c=log lambda+1/m+O(m^(-2)) and a->1/lambda, the exact ceiling gives

    J=theta*m+(2/log lambda)*log m+O(1),
    theta=pi/(2*K*log lambda)=5*pi/(28*log lambda).    (34)

The O(1) includes the exact rounding in (3); it is not an alteration
of the chosen cutoff. Section11 proves0<theta<220/343<1. Hence
1<=J<m for every sufficiently large m and k/m->1-theta>0.
The excluded first theta*m levels and the logarithmic correction are
the activation payment. They are retained in the exponent below.

## 9. Exact products and control of the entire growing index set

For J<m, (8)--(9) give the exact finite identities

    P5_m=1/(1+2*S_(m-1))
       *[a*c^J/(chi*S_J+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2
       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)
          [a*c^j/(chi*S_(j-1)+epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2,  (35)

    P_m=1/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]
       *[(1+a*c^J)/(chi*S_J+2*A+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2
       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)
          [(1+a*c^j)/(chi*S_(j-1)+2*A
                                    +epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2. (36)

The maximum in (31) is EXACTLY epsilon*b^(i), by (9). Products with
no factors are1. In particular a single selected checkpoint retains
the same prefix and tail H_(m-1).

Define the exact scalar corrections

    delta_l=[(1-zeta)*2^l-1]/(zeta*c^l),
    xi_j=1/(a*c^j),
    D_first=chi*(1+delta_J)+epsilon*(c-2)*(1+xi_J),
    D_bulk,j=chi*(1+delta_(j-1))+epsilon*v_mass*(1+xi_j),
    d_first=chi+epsilon*(c-2), d_bulk=chi+epsilon*v_mass.

The denominators D_first,D_bulk,j in this section are scalar
normalizations, distinct from the physical scales D_i in Section5.
Using a/zeta=c-2 and a*c/zeta=v_mass, (35) becomes

    P5_m=(c-2)^2*v_mass^[2*(k-1)]
       /[(1+2*S_(m-1))*D_first^2*product_(j>J)D_bulk,j^2]. (37)

The exceptional first factor is c-2, not v_mass. Replacing its prefix
by H_(J-1) would introduce an erroneous factor tending to lambda^2.

Here c stays in a compact subset of(2,infinity), a and zeta are bounded
away from0, and1-zeta=O(1/m). The EXACT errors obey

    sum_(l>=J)|delta_l|
      <=[(1-zeta)/zeta]*(2/c)^J/(1-2/c)
                         +(1/zeta)*c^(-J)/(1-1/c),
    sum_(j>=J)xi_j=c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)].                (38)

These sums extend to infinity only to upper-bound nonnegative error
terms; no infinite-dimensional function is constructed. By (34) the
bounds vanish exponentially in m. Since d_first,d_bulk>=chi>=1,

    |D_first/d_first-1|<=|delta_J|+epsilon*(c-2)*xi_J,
    |D_bulk,j/d_bulk-1|<=|delta_(j-1)|+epsilon*v_mass*xi_j.

The sum of these bounds, with delta_J counted twice if necessary,
vanishes by (38). Each is eventually at most1/2. The integral formula
for log gives |log(1+t)|<=2|t| there. Therefore replacing all normalized
denominators simultaneously by d_first,d_bulk incurs a total logarithmic
error tending0, explicitly controlled by (38). This is a bound on the
whole product, not a pointwise o(1) multiplied by k without control.

It remains to pay every difference between the actual P_m and P5_m.
For each selected level j, let s^(j)=S_J when j=J and S_(j-1) otherwise,
and put

    V_j=2*A/(chi*s^(j)+epsilon*b_j),
    tau_tail=(1+2*S_(m-1))/[2*(1+S_(m-1))] in(0,1).

The exact ratio is

    P_m/P5_m=tau_tail
                   *product_(j=J)^(m-1)[(1+xi_j)/(1+V_j)]^2. (39)

For every sufficiently large m, (38) gives S_l>=zeta*c^l/2 for l>=J.
The EXACT cutoff (3) gives A*c^(-J)<=epsilon*a. Thus

    sum_j V_j
      <=[4*A/(zeta*chi)]*c^(-J)*[1+1/(1-1/c)]
      <=[4*epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)].           (40)

The separate1 in the bracket pays the first prefix; the geometric
series pays every later gap, including the repeated H_J. Since
-log tau_tail=log[1+1/(1+2*S_(m-1))]<=1/(1+2*S_(m-1)) and
0<=log(1+t)<=t for t>=0, (38)--(40) give

    |log(P_m/P5_m)|
      <=1/(1+2*S_(m-1))
        +2*c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)]
        +[8*epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)] ->0.      (41)

This pays the source-format additive2*A, b versus b-1, and its distinct
terminal factor. Its last term is O(1/m); the other terms decay
exponentially. No checkpoint loss is silently dropped.

## 10. The exact limit, with the first record and terminal block retained

Set gamma=4+2sqrt(2), q_rc=2*gamma. Elementary expansions with uniform
remainders, obtained from the log integral as in Section8, give

    log v_mass=gamma/m+O(m^(-2)),
    log chi=pi*K/(2*m)+O(m^(-2)),
    log(chi+epsilon*v_mass)=(pi*K/2+E)/m+O(m^(-2)).   (42)

The quantities depend only on m, not on the selected level. There
are at most m factors, so summing all O(m^(-2)) remainders gives
O(1/m). Equations (37)--(38) therefore give

    P5_m=(c-2)^2/[(1+2*S_(m-1))*d_first^2]
                 *[v_mass/d_bulk]^[2*(k-1)]*exp(o(1)). (43)

The o(1) is bounded by the explicit sums in (38). Moreover
d_first->1, c-2->lambda^(-1), zeta->1, and (7) gives

    lambda^m/(1+2*S_(m-1)) -> lambda*exp(-1)/2.        (44)

To verify (44), divide S_(m-1) by zeta*c^(m-1); its error delta_(m-1)
tends0 by (38). Then use (lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m)->exp(-1).
The logarithm of the growing power in (43) tends, by (34),(42), to

    (q_rc-pi*K-2*E)*(1-theta).                       (45)

The replacement k=(1-theta)*m+O(log m) contributes O(log(m)/m) to
this logarithm. Combined with (1), (41), and (43)--(45), this proves

    lim B_(n_m)*P_m
      =Z_1/(2*lambda)*exp[-1+(q_rc-pi*K-2*E)*(1-theta)]
      =L_joint.                                     (46)

Every geometric/product error has been summed: it is O(log(m)/m)
apart from exponentially decaying terms before applying the accepted
benchmark convergence. Only the already accepted convergence in (1)
is needed, so no new convergence rate for B_n is assumed or computed.
The factor1/(2*lambda) includes the exceptional first record and the
true terminal child. The enclosing beta cancels exactly through
a/zeta=c-2; it was not separately approximated in each of k factors.

## 11. An exact strict margin from accepted endpoints and rational bounds

The certified table fixed in Section8 of accepted04219554e41d9750 is

The source-format alternative in that Section9 is satisfied with exact

additional per-transfer factor. Thus its limiting loss is ell_loss=0.
The costs are C_eff=7*pi/5, alpha_eff=5*pi/28 and

meets the required activation exponent. The sufficient radius/loss
budget is r*exp(2*ell_loss)=1<1001/1000, where r=lim r_m=1.

One can also match its PRIMARY conditions G2--G3 exactly, rather than

in (39)--(40). On the same physical trajectory define bookkeeping
scales hatD_i=D_i for i<k and hatD_k=sqrt(tau_tail)*D_k. Then

    2*F_m(x_(n_m))=hatD_k^2/(1+2*S_(m-1)).            (53)

For i<k-1 set eta_i=1+V_(J+i), and set
eta_(k-1)=(1+V_(m-1))/sqrt(tau_tail). They are all at least1.
Because b_j=(b_j-1)*(1+xi_j), the exact transfers (20) imply

    hatD_(i+1)>=hatD_i*(b^(i)-1)
       /[(chi*s_i+max{epsilon*b^(i),A})*eta_i].       (54)

This also covers k=1. Every eta is attached to a scale on the actual
path; the final adjustment changes bookkeeping, not the function,

    0<=sum_i log eta_i
       <=sum_j V_j-(1/2)*log tau_tail ->0.             (55)

Thus G1 is Section5 and (27); G2 is (54)--(55); G3 is (53) with
L restored; G4 is dist(e_0,argmin f_m)^2<=1=hatD_0^2. All four
conditions are proved with the actual costs and accumulated losses.
The source construction is not falsely asserted to dominate P5_m
without these finite corrections.

## 14. Final prescribed-word theorem and eventual strict separation

THEOREM (joint analytical candidate; UNVERIFIED). Let lambda=1+sqrt(2).
For each integer m>1 set beta_m=1-1/m, c_m=lambda*(1+1/m),
a_m=beta_m/c_m, H_0=(), and

    H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m*c_m^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,

keeping the SAME m in all children; let n_m=2^m-1. Let B_n and U_n^ZJ
be exactly the original all-split benchmark defined in Section1.
Use the fixed geometric parameters kappa_m=14/(5*m),
epsilon_m=1/(100*m), costs (2), and the exact cutoff (3), selecting
only the first-occurrence records at levels J,...,m-1.

There exists a finite integer M such that for EVERY integer m>=M and
EVERY L>0 the function f_m=L*F_m constructed in (17),(20),(21) has all
the following properties. Its finite dimension is m-J+1. It is convex,
differentiable and has L-Lipschitz gradient. It attains its minimum0
at the actual minimizer0. Starting from the explicit unit vector e_0,
actual GD with EVERY step of H_m, in its original physical order and
with steps h_t/L, has normalized terminal error

    2*(f_m(x_(n_m))-f_m(0))/(L*||e_0-0||^2)
       =P_m>(1+1/1000)*U_(n_m)^ZJ.                    (56)

The exact normalized value P_m is (4), and its exact benchmark-scaled
limit is

    lim B_(n_m)*P_m
      =Z_1/(2*lambda)*exp[-1+
          (8+4sqrt(2)-14*pi/5-1/50)
                *(1-5*pi/(28*log lambda))]
      >5107867121/5100000000>1001/1000.               (57)

The closest-minimizer radius is rho_m=dist(e_0,C_min) in(0,1], with
C_min explicitly given by (29). Its true-distance normalized payoff
is P_m/rho_m^2>=P_m. In particular

    liminf B_(n_m)*W_(n_m)(H_m)>=L_joint>1001/1000.     (58)

PROOF. Section8 establishes J<m for all sufficiently large m, with
the exact paid cutoff. Sections3--6 construct the single static
function, prove its claimed smoothness, minimum, both radius statements,
every physical update, every ordered interpolation row, and the exact
payoff (4). Sections9--10 bound all accumulated errors and give (57).
Section11 proves its strict rational lower enclosure. Section12 checks
that no cost is lost in joining the geometry to the scalar estimate.

To pass from the limit to the quantifier in (56), put
eta_margin=(L_joint-1001/1000)/2>0. By (46), after one finite depth
every subsequent m satisfies |B_(n_m)*P_m-L_joint|<eta_margin. Hence
B_(n_m)*P_m>(L_joint+1001/1000)/2>1001/1000. Enlarge that depth to
include m>=6 and J<m. Multiplication by L leaves the path and payoff
unchanged, so this single M works for every L>0. No numerical value
for M is asserted. The closest-radius comparison gives (58).

This completes the assigned prescribed-family derivation as ONE
UNVERIFIED object. It establishes no optimality over checkpoint sets,
no negative resolution of the minimax problem, and no uniform all-n,
all-nonnegative-word affirmative theorem. The original target remains
OPEN; no new verified fact or automatic worker-round renewal is claimed.
